The loader source SHA-256 is `48b65fb95b088276bd4a0d5db19e3dcbc7e74df4a0f37c9f1a09ad20cba6a617`; line attribution is AI-service commit `6dfa21e` (2026-07-22). This unrestricted deserialization can execute pickle payload code if the file is replaced with an untrusted artifact; this observation does not allege the current file is malicious or that the path was executed in the observed service session.
- The current `substitution_subgraph.pt` is ignored by `models/.gitignore` and absent from Git's tracked-file inventory, while `hgat_weights.pt` is tracked. The loader itself documents a Kaggle-output path and says the ignored subgraph is required to load the HGAT model. A source search across the AI-service repository found no checked-in producer/install script that establishes the ignored file's provenance; the documented `install_kaggle_weights.py` was not present in the searched tree.
- The adjacent HGAT state-dict load uses `weights_only=True`; a repository-wide Python-source sweep found that as the only other `torch.load` call under the scanned workspace roots. The separate research tooling has nine `pickle.load` sites across seven scripts for local vocabulary/split artifacts; `run_recipe_context_dual_encoder.py` checks pickle globals before loading, while the other sites were not audited for equivalent guards. These are not the serving loader, and their trust boundaries were not audited in this pass.
- **Disposition / next acceptance:** preserve the binary and registry unchanged. M-A remains OPEN; R39/R40 stay quarantined. Before any deployment or model inference, establish an authenticated provenance path for the ignored subgraph and replace or tightly constrain unrestricted deserialization (for example, an audited restricted-load allowlist plus exact artifact hash verification); add regression coverage and verify the real serving path. Evidence: `evidence/lead-ma-ai-service-deserialization-lineage-20261021.json`.

## LEAD v332 — Add independent report evidence for the cited Thai cookbook's provenance

- **Independent bibliographic/provenance lead:** a 2011 final report hosted in the Thai Research Fund's electronic library says a book titled “อาหารไทยจากอดีตสู่ปัจจุบัน” (“Thai food from the past to the present”) was launched in B.E. 2552 (2009), as a collaboration among the National Food Institute, Suan Dusit Rajabhat University, and Mahidol University's Institute of Nutrition. It describes the book as arising from a named standard-recipe project for foreign tourists and reports 20 adapted recipe standards. See the [full report](https://elibrary.tsri.or.th/fullP/RDG5350052/RDG5350052_full.pdf), §2.7, printed p. 43 (PDF p. 66). The report's English rendering of the project title reads “Standard Recipes of Thai Popular Dished for Foreigners” [sic].
- **Why this matters / evidence boundary:** this independently supports a 2009 launch and a 20-recipe provenance trail for a book with the same Thai title as CoO-RL's cited NFI book, making the paper's “NFI (2008)” plus “Se-ed Promotion Company Limited (1997)” imprint/date combination a concrete edition-resolution question. The report does not reproduce the cited book's title/imprint page, establish that the NFI-hosted `2564_6_Thaifood.pdf` is the same edition or exact 20-recipe dataset, or show that CoO-RL used the project recipes. Matching title and recipe count are not identity proof; no book/PDF bytes or hash were obtained.
- **Next acceptance:** compare the authorized NFI file's title/imprint and contents with the report's named project and CoO-RL's recipe names/ingredients. Preserve/hash authorized bytes, document row-level correspondence and transformations, and verify reuse terms independently. Keep exact edition, dataset lineage, mapping, and rights `UNKNOWN` until those checks pass; do not silently “correct” the CoO-RL citation to 2009.

## LEAD v336 — Tie CoO-RL's recipe set to the NFI book's cataloged modern-recipe section

The English record exposes a TOC with separate “traditional” and “modern” recipe sections. Its ordered 20-item modern section aligns with CoO-RL Table VII's ordered 20 recipe labels; the Thai Research Fund report's 20 adapted recipes likewise track that modern set. It supports a strong likely-lineage hypothesis that CoO-RL's 20-recipe set corresponds to the book's *modern* set, but it remains catalog-TOC evidence, not inspection of the book's ingredient quantities or proof of the paper's actual extraction path. Specific labels diverge: the book TOC describes modern item 10 as chicken/beans with curry paste, while CoO-RL says “Stir Fried Chicken with Ginger”; the book describes item 20 as mock-pomegranate seeds with syrup/coconut milk, while CoO-RL says “Water Chestnuts in Syrup Coconut.” Resolve whether these are translations, source/version changes, or paper-label errors. The anomalous “Se-ed … (1997)” clause is still unexplained; no artifact was hashed and no reuse permission was established.
- **Next acceptance:** inspect an authorized ISBN-matched copy (TCDC locates the English edition in its Bangkok general collection) and compare the modern-section recipe pages, ingredients, quantities, and transformations against the report and CoO-RL graph. Verify the FCT as the separate nutrient source, preserve/hash permitted source artifacts, reconcile the citation/Se-ed note, and verify rights. Upgrade lineage only after item-level artifact comparison; retain `UNKNOWN` otherwise.

It materially narrows the retrieval target behind the v329 search lead and is consistent with the official derivative citation in v330, but does not prove the file equals the edition/data used by CoO-RL or map any of its recipes to table rows.

Record reuse terms separately; keep the original-file, mapping, and permission gates open until verified.

Do not merge their results or declare duplication.

## LEAD v326 — Expand taxonomy-companion evidence without promoting its abstract (2026-10-15)

- **Abstract-level content:** the surfaced [author-profile record](https://independent.academia.edu/WanvimolNadee) for “Thai Ingredient Replacement Recommendation using Taxonomy” describes ingredient taxonomy, ingredient characteristics, DBSCAN, and a similarity function; it says the approach was evaluated on Thai recipes against prior alternative-ingredient work, including through a cooking-and-tasting experiment, and reports improved recommendation-list satisfaction in appropriateness and compatibility. Maejo's author pages independently identify the same paper and link to IEEE (LEAD v325). No full paper bytes were obtained in this screen.
- **Evidence ceiling:** these are abstract/profile-level method and author-reported outcome statements, not a full-text protocol. Dataset/version, candidate and split construction, participant/sample counts, rating units/denominators, raw results, uncertainty, evaluator blinding, case overlap with the prior study, and allergen-safety validation remain unknown. “Cooking and tasting” is not by itself proof of a specific safety, statistical, or independent-replication claim.
- **Disposition / next acceptance:** retain the taxonomy paper as distinct Thai-recipe prior art; do not count its abstract as a completed full-text screen or independent validation. Obtain/hash authorized full text, then compare dataset lineage and case/evaluator reuse with the correlation-weight paper and predecessor study before upgrading L42 status.

## LEAD v324 — Bound the publisher-record supplement search (2026-10-15)

The article names the corresponding author and gives an email address, but this screen did not contact the author or publisher.

## LEAD v323 — Clarify the unresolved CoO-RL user-study denominator (2026-10-15)

- **Primary-text check:** In the evaluation section, CoO-RL says a user study involved 30 participants, compares CF/CBF/proposed method, and defines a recommendation as correct when a participant gives it a score of 3–5 (scores 1–2 are incorrect). Table XVIII reports 73.33%, 76.67%, and 80.00% only; it does not report raw correct/incorrect counts, the number of ratings or recommendations per participant and method, aggregation order, or uncertainty. See the [publisher-hosted full text](https://www.jait.us/articles/2025/JAIT-V16N3-318.pdf), evaluation §IV and Table XVIII.
- **Arithmetic is not protocol evidence:** the percentages are numerically compatible with 22/30, 23/30, and 24/30 if—and only if—the denominator were one binary outcome per participant per method. The paper does not establish that denominator; multiple recommendation ratings or another aggregation could produce the same rounded percentages. Do not reconstruct counts, confidence intervals, or a paired comparison from the reported percentages.
- **Keep study components separate:** the four Pad Thai scenarios are presented as demonstrations and are not identified as the N=30 study's rating set. The N=30 label establishes participant count, not the independent rating unit or test-case count. The article itself calls for validation on larger and more diverse datasets; neither that statement nor the reported percentages is independent replication or allergen-safety validation.
- **Disposition / next acceptance:** keep the outcomes strictly author-reported; the denominator, rating unit, per-person aggregation, case set, and uncertainty remain `UNKNOWN`. L42 and the relevant crosswalk row remain open. Resolve only from an author/publisher clarification or an authorized protocol/raw-results artifact, then record its provenance; do not treat a denominator inferred from percentage increments as verified.

[35], both HTTPS connections failed before receiving a response: curl exit 28, HTTP 000, 0 downloaded bytes (about 21 seconds to connect for each). It does not show either source was deleted or globally inaccessible, and it yields no document hash, content, license, or data-lineage evidence. The official DoH table description, INMU distinctions/terms, and the CoO-RL bibliography remain as scoped in v321.

## LEAD v321 — Bound CoO-RL's cited Thai source versions and reuse-rights evidence (2026-10-15)

- **What the cited sources establish:** CoO-RL reference [36] names the Bureau of Nutrition, Department of Health, Ministry of Public Health's 2018 “Table showing the nutritional value of Thai food.” The official Thai nutrition agency's material describes its table as Thai foods with nutrient amounts per 100 g and links a file named `ตารางคุณค่า 2018.pdf`; the PDF endpoint did not open in this screen, so no table bytes/hash or item-level entries were verified. CoO-RL reference [35] names an NFI 2008 book, “Thai food from the past to the present,” with an NFI PDF URL; that endpoint timed out. The paper's citation also pairs “2008” with “Se-ed Promotion Company Limited (1997),” and the linked path contains `2564` (Thai year 2021), leaving the exact book edition/file version to verify rather than silently normalize.
- **Do not conflate the other Thai database:** Mahidol University's Institute of Nutrition (INMU) exposes an online Thai Food Composition Database, including a version dated September 2018; its current version 3 is dated August 2025 and integrates/revises later sources. INMU explicitly reserves rights, permits free access/use for non-commercial users with attribution, and says reproduction/distribution/other uses require permission; commercial or educational-product use may incur fees. This is a different publisher/institutional data product from the Department of Health 2018 table cited by CoO-RL. INMU terms do not establish rights for the Department of Health table, and the Mahidol database is not a proven replacement for the cited 2018 source.
- **Lineage/rights disposition:** this screen identifies the two sources CoO-RL cites and establishes a version/rights verification dependency; it does not establish that the authors' 20-recipe graph was derived from all records in either source, how recipe rows/nutrients were selected or transformed, or whether the source licenses permit the paper's dataset construction or downstream reuse. No rights conclusion is made. Keep claims of nutrition preservation separate from source availability and from validated nutrient calculations.
- **Next acceptance:** obtain the exact CoO-RL source files/table version through authorized routes; retain hashes and edition metadata; map the 20 recipes and nutrient fields to source rows and transformation steps; record source-specific license/permission before reuse. If the publisher-author paper only lists sources without this mapping, keep data lineage and rights `UNKNOWN`; do not substitute the current Mahidol database.

## LEAD v320 — Pin the CoO-RL Thai-paper citation chain without implying replication (2026-10-15)

- **Primary identity:** IEEE Xplore identifies Nadee and Unankard, “Alternative-Ingredient Recommendation Based on Correlation Weight for Thai Recipes,” DOI [`10.1109/ECTIDAMTNCON51128.2021.9425778`](https://doi.org/10.1109/ECTIDAMTNCON51128.2021.9425778) ([IEEE record](https://ieeexplore.ieee.org/document/9425778)). The proceedings contents list it at p. 14; Maejo's author-university record gives pp. 14–17 and publication date 6 March 2021. IEEE full text did not render in this screen; ResearchGate marks no full text available. No article bytes/hash or code/data repository were obtained. A bounded title/DOI/repository search did not surface an author code repository; that is not proof none exists.
- **Direct citation-chain evidence:** CoO-RL's publisher-hosted full text cites this 2021 correlation-weight paper as reference [22] and Nadee, Unankard, and TeCho's 2021 taxonomy paper as reference [23], alongside Yamanishi et al. 2015 as [21]. Its §II.E says the Thai work uses correlation metrics/weighting and a Thai recipe database; §III says CoO-RL draws on a Thai recipe book and Thai Food Composition Table and combines graph/co-occurrence/ontology methods. This establishes that CoO-RL presents the Thai works as related antecedents, not that the 2025 study reuses their data, cases, or implementation, or independently replicates them.
- **Disposition:** keep the two Nadee papers separate from each other and from CoO-RL; retain method/evaluation details for the correlation-weight paper as `UNKNOWN` until its authorized full text is screened. No repository data/code rights, overlap, benchmark denominator, allergen validation, or independent replication is established. This narrows source identity and the authors' own citation framing only; it does not close L42/L48 or change novelty `UNKNOWN`, R28/R29 `UNVERIFIED`, SSoT, claims register, experiment, safety, or thesis status.

## LEAD v319 — Screen CoO-RL full text and bound its reported evaluation (2026-10-15)

- **Source/provenance:** Screened Nataporn Thammabunwarit et al., “Co-occurrence and Ontology Reinforcement Learning: CoO-RL in Food Recommendations,” *Journal of Advances in Information Technology* 16(3), 318–329 (2025), DOI [`10.12720/jait.16.3.318-329`](https://doi.org/10.12720/jait.16.3.318-329), from the [publisher record](https://www.jait.us/show-251-1656-1.html) and [publisher-hosted PDF](https://www.jait.us/articles/2025/JAIT-V16N3-318.pdf). The publisher labels it CC BY 4.0. The downloaded PDF was 3,007,361 bytes, SHA-256 `a998f84ee0b17d874129091fecba9bbd00b8392e8e322ebaf0179f9f0db03959`; the temporary copy was removed after screening, so these bytes are not retained locally.
- **Task/data/method as described:** the paper reports a Thai cookbook and food-composition source, with a graph of 20 recipes, 70 ingredients, and 24 nutrients, and reports 936,520 relations. It combines co-occurrence, ontology/category information, profile constraints, and feedback. The displayed method describes recipe/current-ingredient/profile states, `{Update, Rebuild, Nothing}` actions, an ingredient-relation score, a score-per-edge reward with satisfaction/dissatisfaction signs, and updating `ingredient_contra`. Treat all counts, mechanics, and outcomes here as paper-reported, not independently reproduced. The displayed equations do not fully specify a conventional Q-learning transition/update procedure or its learning parameters; this is a reproducibility gap in the presented method, not a categorical claim that the system is not reinforcement learning.
- **Evaluation ceiling:** the paper describes four simulated scenarios for one shrimp-allergy user and a separate user study of 30 participants comparing CF (73.33%), CBF (76.67%), and CoO-RL (80.00%); “correct” is defined as participant scores 3–5. It does not clearly specify the rating unit/denominator, raw counts, per-person aggregation, held-out pair protocol, or uncertainty in the screened account. Do not translate 80% into a count or benchmark/generalization claim. The authors identify larger and more diverse data validation as future work.
- **Safety and nutrition boundary:** the example removes shrimp and dried shrimp, while listed candidates include boiled squid and grated fish fillets. The screened paper does not establish a validated allergen-screening, cross-contact, or clinical-safety process; these examples are not safety evidence. Although nutrient information appears in the dataset description and nutrient maintenance is claimed, no explicit nutrient-preservation metric or validation was identified in the screened experiment. Do not infer suitability for an individual allergy or nutrition needs.
- **Lineage/disposition:** the references list Yamanishi et al. (2015), Shino et al. (2016), and Nadee et al. (2021) as separate works. Bibliographic linkage supports literature adjacency, not proof of shared data/cases, exact method lineage, or independent replication. This is useful direct prior art for ontology/co-occurrence-based food substitution, but it does not establish ChefKix's frozen Recipe1MSubs unseen-pair estimand, leakage-controlled benchmark, calibrated abstention, or validated safety guardrail. L42/L48 remain open; novelty `UNKNOWN`, R28/R29 `UNVERIFIED`; no SSoT, claims-register, rights, experiment, safety, or thesis status changed.
- **Next acceptance:** if authorized code/data are available, pin and inspect their exact versions and rights; trace the rating unit, denominator, and Thai recipe/candidate construction; compare the cited Thai papers' datasets and cases. Keep author-reported results distinct from independently reproduced evidence and make no safety inference.

## LEAD v316 — Screen the 2015 Shino-lineage method and bound its small cooking evaluation (2026-10-15)

- **Text/provenance:** screened the rendered full text of Yamanishi et al., “Alternative-ingredient Recommendation Based on Co-occurrence Relation on Recipe Database,” *Procedia Computer Science* 60 (2015), 986–993, DOI [`10.1016/j.procs.2015.08.138`](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.procs.2015.08.138), through its [ResearchGate public-full-text page](https://www.researchgate.net/publication/281412809_Alternative-ingredient_Recommendation_Based_on_Co-occurrence_Relation_on_Recipe_Database); cross-checked title, venue, DOI, and open-access status against the [Elsevier record](https://www.sciencedirect.com/science/article/pii/S1877050915022656). The shared page displays the paper's CC BY-NC-ND 4.0 notice and printed pages. Its PDF-download route returned 403, so the text was screened in-browser but no exact PDF bytes were retained or hashed; record this as a rendered-text screen, not a hash-verified acquisition.
- **Method/data:** §3 uses 4,508 randomly selected recipes from an 18,650-recipe “Kyou no Ryori”/NHK recipe site corpus, with a subjective seasoning dictionary to exclude seasoners; this is not a Cookpad corpus. Algorithm 1 ranks compatibility with the recipe's remaining ingredients using a Naive-Bayes-style co-occurrence score and explicitly does not use the exchanged ingredient. Algorithm 2 represents an ingredient's role by mutual-information relations to the remaining ingredients and ranks candidates by Hamming distance. The authors acknowledge that the method does not filter ingredient types: pork and clams appear among burdock alternatives; ingredient categorization is future work. The paper's allergy language is motivation only: its target cases were selected because preferences differed, no allergen constraint was tested, and allergy/nutrient factors for a hospital-diet system are future work.
- **Evaluation denominator / ceiling:** the paper cooks 12 selected replacements across two recipes (5 Chikuzen-ni/burdock and 7 green-curry-risotto/grape-tomato candidates); one author cooked them, and five subjects rated them. Table 5 contains 35 Algorithm-1 ratings and 25 Algorithm-2 ratings: A/B/C (the paper's acceptable group) totals 32/35 = 91.4% and 23/25 = 92.0%, respectively, or 55/60 = 91.7% combined. This reconciles the authors' “over 90% acceptable” statement to its actual rating denominator. It is not 90% of recipe pairs, a held-out benchmark, an independent replication, a blinded/calibrated judge study, or allergen/clinical validation; the paper leaves comparison with collaborative filtering as future work.
- **Lineage/disposition:** the 2015 text provides direct prior art for co-occurrence-based substitute ranking with recipe context. The related 2016 record adds ingredient-category/ontology wording, and the Springer 2018 chapter abstract reports that combination and an 88% figure, but exact data, cases, evaluator, and version relationship remain unverified. Keep the records distinct and do not count them as independent replications. This satisfies v315's 2015 text-screening action only at rendered-text level; retain the exact-byte/hash gap. L42/L48 remain open; novelty `UNKNOWN`, R28/R29 `UNVERIFIED`; no register, SSoT, rights, experiment, safety, or thesis status changed.
- **Next acceptance:** obtain/pin an authorized exact 2015 PDF if available; acquire the 2016 proceedings and Springer chapter through authorized access and compare corpus, algorithms/category handling, case construction, rating denominator, participant/evaluator identity, and result deltas. Do not infer reuse from shared authors or the 88% abstract figure; do not claim safety from motivation language or recipe tasting.

## LEAD v313 — Full-text screen of Shino et al.'s multi-aspect substitution method (2026-10-15)

- **Primary source:** screened the complete J-STAGE article by Shino, Yamanishi, and Nishihara, “Discovery of Relationships Among Ingredients Based on Various Aspects for Recommending Alternative Ingredients—Focusing on Affinity, Role, and Texture,” *Journal of Japan Society for Fuzzy Theory and Intelligent Informatics*, 30(6), 779–787 (2018), DOI [`10.3156/jsoft.30.6_779`](https://doi.org/10.3156/jsoft.30.6_779); [publisher-hosted full text](https://www.jstage.jst.go.jp/article/jsoft/30/6/30_779/_pdf/-char/ja). This advances the 2018 item beyond v299's abstract-level record. The PDF was screened in-session, not retained or locally hashed.
- **Method and data boundary:** the article proposes three separate recommendation views: affinity to the recipe's remaining ingredients (recipe co-occurrence with a Naive-Bayes-style score), role similarity (word2vec over ingredient-list order/co-occurrence), and texture similarity (word2vec over ingredient names and texture terms in recipe reviews). It reports using the Cookpad/NII researcher dataset described as 1.72 million recipes through 2014-09-30 and a Japanese cooking ontology for ingredient-name normalization. The experiment uses six selected recipes, each with one designated exchange ingredient; this is direct prior art for data-driven, context-aware alternative-ingredient recommendation, including multiple recommendation criteria.
- **Evaluation ceiling:** three non-author participants in their twenties who cooked at least weekly took part in the subjective checks. For affinity, the paper also searches Cookpad for top-ranked candidates and reports 59/60 with a matching-category recipe versus 42/60 for random candidates; 80 candidates without a found matching recipe receive subjective ratings, with 41/80 judged good by at least two of three participants. Role and texture checks compare sampled top/middle/bottom candidates across the six recipes. These small, selected checks are not a held-out unseen-pair benchmark, independent replication, calibrated judge study, or clinical/allergen-safety validation. Allergy appears as motivation/context, not a tested safety outcome.
- **Disposition:** direct prior art narrows any broad claim that recipe-context or multi-criterion ingredient substitution is absent. The paper does not establish the frozen ChefKix Recipe1MSubs split/estimand, leakage-controlled unseen-pair performance, selective calibration/risk–coverage, provenance-bearing abstention, or validated guardrails. It cites the related Shino et al. 2016 work; version/experiment overlap with that work and the previously screened Yamanishi lineage remain unresolved, so do not count them as independent replications. Keep exact novelty `UNKNOWN`, R28/R29 `UNVERIFIED`, and L42/L48 open. No SSoT, source register, claims status, artifact/rights audit, experiment, or thesis claim changed.
- **L42 next acceptance:** create a version-specific source entry with publisher DOI, full-text access state, and provenance; compare the 2015/2016/2018 related records for dataset, candidate construction, recipe context, split/holdout, human evaluation, and version overlap. Then audit accessible artifacts/rights and obtain independent exact-task adjudication. Do not infer unseen-pair validation or safety from the reported subjective checks.

## LEAD v310 — Clarify the Shidochi 2011 evaluation denominator and bibliography (2026-10-15)

- **Evaluation denominator correction:** the author-hosted full text of the 2011 IEICE article says one nutritionist-qualified evaluator assessed 53 recipes without being told which had substitutions: 13 originals plus 40 one-ingredient substitutions, with quantity descriptions removed. All 13 originals received label (1), but the reported substitution table covers only the 40 modified recipes: 15 label (1), 15 label (2), one label (3), and nine label (4). Thus the paper's 75% is 30/40 modified recipes combining replacement-and-action appropriate with replacement-appropriate/action-inappropriate; only 15/40 were rated fully appropriate including the cooking action. Do not describe the result as 75% of all 53 recipes or as 75% action-valid substitutions. This sharpens v303's summary; it does not add an independent study or validation.
- **Lineage corroboration:** the same 2011 article's reference list cites the 2008 JSAI paper, the CEA'09 paper, and Karasawa's 66th IPSJ convention item as March 2004. This is primary-source corroboration of the cited lineage and supports the bibliographic match recorded in v308, while leaving the 2008 paper's printed 2006 discrepancy visible and the precise version/content deltas unresolved.
- **Evidence boundary:** the source reports one evaluator, no quantity descriptions during rating, no held-out substitute-pair validation, and only a small actual-cooking/tasting illustration. It explicitly leaves user experience/knowledge/preferences and other-ingredient compatibility relevant. These findings do not establish allergen safety, generalization, or the frozen ChefKix estimand. Keep exact novelty `UNKNOWN`, R28/R29 `UNVERIFIED`, and L42/L48 open; no experiment, artifact/rights audit, claims-register status, canonical strategy, thesis claim, or training state changed.
- **L42 next acceptance:** compare the 2008, 2009, and 2011 protocols at source level, including whether the same 40 modified recipes and labels were reused; keep the 13 originals outside the substitution-result denominator; then continue the exact-task crosswalk and independent adjudication. Source: [2011 IEICE full text](https://www.vislab.is.i.nagoya-u.ac.jp/~murase/en/pdf/665-pdf.pdf), especially §§3.2 and references, pp. 534–535.

The table discrepancy is affirmative evidence of a published result delta; it could reflect a changed candidate, different case/result, or editorial correction, and the articles do not resolve which. The identity of the evaluator across versions and case-level overlap remain `UNKNOWN`. Replace “strongly indicating overlapping/reused evaluation” with “possible shared or revised evaluation; exact reuse unverified.” This also supersedes v310's categorical wording that the 2011 paper adds no independent study: independence is not established either. No experiment, artifact/rights audit, claims-register status, canonical strategy, thesis claim, or training state changed.

## LEAD v297 — Screen allergy-aware recipe-context substitution lineage for L42 (2026-10-15)

- **Primary-source lineage:** the official SBC record for Pacifico, Britto, and Ludermir's WebMedia '21 paper, “Ingredient Substitute Recommendation Based on Collaborative Filtering and Recipe Context for Automatic Allergy-Safe Recipe Generation,” pp. 97–104 (2021-11-05), exposes an abstract and links the publisher PDF ([proceedings record](https://sol.sbc.org.br/index.php/webmedia/article/view/17483); [PDF endpoint](https://sol.sbc.org.br/index.php/webmedia/article/download/17483/17318)). The abstract describes single-ingredient substitution conditioned on recipe context, ingredient-set relationships, and user food-restriction/preferences, with qualitative analysis. The linked PDF returned HTTP 403 in this pass; no full-text claim is made for that paper.
- **Earlier full-text screen:** Oliveira, Britto, Pacífico, and Ludermir's ENIAC 2019 paper, “Recipe Recommendation and Generation Based on Ingredient Substitution,” was read from the official [SBC proceedings record](https://sol.sbc.org.br/index.php/eniac/article/view/9287) and [12-page PDF](https://sol.sbc.org.br/index.php/eniac/article/download/9287/9189), DOI `10.5753/eniac.2019.9287`. It proposes a Brazilian-Portuguese corpus (3,106 recipes, 1,312 ingredients) and single-ingredient replacement using recipe/ingredient similarity, co-occurrence and ingredient categories. Its filtering includes a prohibited-ingredient list derived from zero pair co-occurrence, which the authors interpret as potentially toxic or unpalatable; this is a data heuristic, not a source-backed allergen rule. The reported evaluation is qualitative: two example recipes, three target ingredients each, and Top-5 candidates. The paper explicitly leaves deeper weight analysis and nutritional/flavor analysis for future work.
- **Cited 2015 direct-prior-art boundary:** Ooi, Iiba, and Takano's PACRIM 2015 paper has DOI `10.1109/PACRIM.2015.7334878`, pp. 444–449 (bibliographic record verified through Crossref). Its abstract, available through a secondary catalog and summarized by the 2019 paper, describes allergy-safe substitute suggestion from recipe similarity/food context. IEEE Xplore returned HTTP 418 in this check and Crossref carries no abstract; no full text was screened. Treat this as a separate allergy-aware prior-art lineage at abstract/citation level, not an independent full-text review or safety validation.
- **Full-text follow-up:** the same author group’s official SBC KDMiLe 2022.2 paper, “Automatic Recipe Ingredient Substitution Based on Text Mining and Data Clustering Approaches,” was read from its [eight-page proceedings PDF](https://sol.sbc.org.br/index.php/kdmile/article/download/24972/24793). It uses Food.com/Recipe1M-derived recipe data and three target restriction domains (diabetes, gluten-free, lactose-free), proposes single-ingredient replacement, and combines a K-means recipe-context/ingredient-acceptance filter with a target-ingredient pair-occurrence filter. The reported qualitative check ranks candidates for four example recipes, uses external web references to judge examples, and selects filter weights after trial-and-error. This is direct prior art for data-driven, recipe-context candidate substitution and restriction-domain adaptation; the study itself describes its evaluation as qualitative.
- **Evidence ceiling and safety boundary:** the 2019 and 2022 PDFs are primary text but were streamed for reading, not retained in the workspace or hashed; no implementation/data artifact or rights/license audit, independent reproduction, held-out unseen-pair benchmark, participant study, or clinical validation was performed. The small qualitative example sets and author-described safety language do not establish real-world allergen safety. The WebMedia '21 and Ooi 2015 papers remain abstract/citation-level with full texts blocked. Treat the Oliveira/Pacifico/Britto/Ludermir 2019–2022 works as one related author lineage, not independent replications; keep Ooi's distinct 2015 author group separate.
- **Exact-task boundary:** none of these inspected records establishes the frozen candidate's Recipe1MSubs unseen-pair estimand, selective calibration/risk–coverage, evidence-missingness strata, provenance-bearing abstention, or judge-to-human calibration. The 2019 zero-cooccurrence list is not equivalent to an authoritative allergy ontology or validated fail-closed guardrail. This evidence defeats broad absence claims about recipe-context/restriction-aware ingredient substitution, but neither proves nor refutes novelty for ChefKix's exact combination. Keep this candidate provisional/unmapped pending authorized SSoT source-register reconciliation; do not invent an Appendix C ID or conflate it with S23/Vij/SafePlates.

## LEAD v302 — Screen cooking-action-aware ingredient replacement prior art (2026-10-15)

- **Primary full-text record:** Shidochi, Takahashi, Ide, and Murase, “Finding Replaceable Materials in Cooking Recipe Texts Considering Characteristic Cooking Actions,” ACM Multimedia 2009 Workshop on Multimedia for Cooking and Eating Activities (CEA'09), pp. 9–14, DOI `10.1145/1630995.1630998`. The [Nagoya University laboratory-hosted full text](https://www.vislab.is.i.nagoya-u.ac.jp/~murase/en/pdf/376-pdf.pdf) identifies the venue, date, authors, and DOI; the [author laboratory publication list](https://www.cs.is.i.nagoya-u.ac.jp/users/ide/res/publication-e.html) independently lists the same citation. This is a stable institutional copy, not an ACM-hosted publisher download; no local artifact/hash or rights audit was made.
- **Method and evidence:** the paper groups 6,789 Japanese recipe texts by recipe-name endings, extracts ingredient/action links, and compares characteristic cooking-action vectors; its focused experiment uses 391 recipes, 168 material types, and nine groups to produce 92 candidate pairs. One dietitian-qualified evaluator rated 40 generated recipe texts, with ratings 1 (materials and procedure appropriate) or 2 (materials appropriate, procedure needs action-description changes) combined as 75% “appropriate.” The authors also cooked two pizza-toast variants and report mixed taste comments. These are small author-reported, non-blinded examples, not independent or held-out validation; importantly, the 75% endpoint permits procedure repair and is not a clean substitute-correctness accuracy.
- **Limits and task boundary:** the authors explicitly say simple material replacement can mismatch cooking actions and propose replacing materials and actions together; they also flag recipe-group classification, text-processing, data-size, and preference limitations. This is direct prior art for action-aware, recipe-context replacement with a small human appropriateness check, but not for dietary/allergen constraints, authoritative restriction sources, hard fail-closed filtering, uncertainty calibration, provenance-bearing abstention, the frozen Recipe1MSubs unseen-pair estimand, or judge-to-human calibration. The paper predates the later Japanese “Finding Replaceable Materials by Cooking Recipe Mining” record; whether that is an expanded/versioned continuation remains UNKNOWN until a version-level comparison.
- **Disposition:** broaden L42's direct-prior-art map: contextual action compatibility and human assessment of replacement-generated recipes are not absent. Do not inflate the reported 75% into general accuracy, safety, or proof for the frozen task. Keep exact novelty `UNKNOWN`, R28/R29 `UNVERIFIED`, and L42/L48 open; no experiment, artifact audit, claims-register status, canonical strategy, or thesis claim changed.
- **L42 next acceptance:** add a distinct source/version row; resolve the relationship to the later Japanese paper and follow the cited data-mining baseline to its primary source; compare dataset, pair construction, action adaptation, evaluator rubric, and leakage/holdout status with the frozen protocol; audit artifacts/rights and obtain independent adjudication.

The 2011 source itself cites both 2008 and CEA'09, corroborating citation lineage.

## LEAD v305 — Qualify the Shidochi lineage's characterization of its cited Karasawa antecedent (2026-10-15)

- **Bibliographic discrepancy (unresolved):** the JSAI'08 primary PDF itself prints `[唐沢 06]` and dates the same-title Karasawa, Hamada, Ide, Sakai, and Tanaka item to March **2006**, *IPSJ 66th National Convention Proceedings*, vol. 2, pp. 119–120. In contrast, the [IPSJ institutional-library record](https://ipsj.ixsq.nii.ac.jp/records/169702) dates a same-title, same-author, same-volume/page record to March **2004**; the [Nagoya author profile](https://profs.provost.nagoya-u.ac.jp/html/100000090_ronbn_44_ja.html) and a [Tanaka lab listing](https://lab.iisec.ac.jp/~tanaka_lab/publications/taikai.html) also place the same-title contribution in 2004, although that lab listing gives a different session/page range. Do not collapse these into a settled correction: a citation error, distinct version, or metadata discrepancy remains possible until the proceedings records are reconciled.
- **Task/method boundary:** the 2004 paper mines characteristic ingredients and cooking actions within recipe groups, then proposes ingredient replacement candidates by finding frequently used, non-characteristic ingredients that occur exclusively (do not co-occur) in the same group. Thus it does represent cooking-action knowledge at the recipe-group level, but its reported replacement rule is based on exclusive ingredient occurrence, not an action-vector match for each proposed substitute pair.

A narrower difference remains supportable from this text: Shidochi's candidate rule compares ingredient-linked characteristic-action vectors, whereas Karasawa's candidate rule uses exclusion among common, non-characteristic ingredients after group-level action mining. Whether the 2004 paper is the exact baseline behind the 2008 “few pairs” statement, and whether “action relation” refers to candidate-level rather than group-level treatment, need source-specific adjudication. Do not transfer the claim to the separately cited Ohira et al. 2000 lineage.
- **Disposition:** keep the 2004/2006 identity unresolved and record the screened 2004 text only as a same-title candidate antecedent. Its group-level action mining and exclusive-cooccurrence candidate rule qualify a broad “prior methods omit action” characterization if this is the cited version, but do not establish validity or resolve the frozen task's novelty. Keep exact novelty `UNKNOWN`, R28/R29 `UNVERIFIED`, and L42/L48 open; no experiment, code/data audit, claims-register status, canonical strategy, or thesis claim changed.
- **L42 next acceptance:** reconcile the 2004/2006 Karasawa proceedings identity and obtain the 2000 Ohira et al. paper itself; compare each baseline's actual candidate construction and evaluation separately before narrowing the 2008 criticism. ## LEAD v291 — Screen KDD'26 LLM judge as adjacent evaluation/guardrail prior art (2026-10-15)

- **Method/evaluation boundary:** the paper constructs an LLM-derived semantic preference proxy from user histories and item text, judges each Top-K candidate with a reasoning-then-scoring prompt, requires evidence before acceptance (reject-by-default), retains a rationale, and aggregates judgments into recommendation metrics. It compares proxy ranking consistency with unbiased/full-exposure evaluation on KuaiRec and Coat; the reported results are author claims, not local evidence. This is substantial prior art for generic LLM judge reliability/explainability and conservative relevance gating in recommenders.
- **Exact-task distinction:** this evaluates collaborative-filtering recommendation lists against inferred user preferences; the inspected paper does not study ingredient substitution, substitute-pair identity/generalization, Recipe1MSubs Hit@K, substitution-specific allergen evidence, or a provenance-linked substitute abstention contract. Therefore it narrows generic "first reliable/explainable LLM recommender judge" or "first reject-by-default recommendation gate" claims, but neither establishes nor refutes novelty for ChefKix's frozen substitution estimand. Exact novelty stays `UNKNOWN`.
- **Disposition / acceptance:** add this as a separate direct-adjacent comparator in R28/R29 and L42; do not merge its user-preference evaluation estimand with Amendola's conformal rating intervals or Senath/Rita substitution generators. Keep register rows `UNVERIFIED`. Next compare its prompt/labels, human validation, dataset/split, and judge failure protocol to the frozen protocol; audit the linked implementation at an immutable commit and rights before relying on implementation claims; then obtain independent adjudication. No execution or source-register/SSoT/status change occurred; L42/L48 remain open.

Thus broad "first LLM/SFT+DPO ingredient substitution" wording is not supportable. These are source-reported results, not local reproduction.

It also identifies lower multi-result ranking metrics, missing culturally varied ingredient-name handling, and potential unsafe substitutions; expert review and safety filtering are described as future work. The linked repository is public, but this pass did not inspect notebook/CSV bytes, audit or run code, or reproduce results. These limitations suggest comparison questions; they do not prove ChefKix novelty or safety.

## LEAD v236 — Bind R28/R29 to the full-text Senath SFT+DPO comparator (2026-10-14)

- **Novelty boundary:** this is direct prior art for LLM ingredient substitution on Recipe1MSubs and for the broad SFT/DPO training recipe; it blocks any “first LLM/SFT/DPO on Recipe1MSubs” framing. The article uses Recipe1MSubs and the GISMo train/validation/test split (49,044/10,729/10,747), evaluates recipe-context prompts, trains SFT, uses 7,500 DPO examples, and applies a further substitution fine-tune after DPO. Source: `https://mro.massey.ac.nz/server/api/core/bitstreams/2aa7b382-7d08-46a5-bb26-d3907417fb27/content`; `https://doi.org/10.1016/j.nlp.2025.100177`; `https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.04922`.

Before any novelty claim, compare the article's full methods, data construction, evaluation and public code with the current frozen thesis protocol and training lineage; record the exact overlap/delta, obtain independent review, and retain the evidence ceiling. This comparator neither changes L44's training plan nor validates this project's training, model quality, or inference. Bitbucket's public API currently returns 404 for `recipe-generator`. It identifies the public `ingredient-substitution` repository's `master` at `f29517446dff50a5b1fbfba685c1e781d7f8c50b`, commit date 2024-11-07, message “Models and data added.” The current tree inventory contains model-arm folders, train/validation/test JSONL files, prediction outputs, notebooks, category data, and a README. Sources: [journal article](https://www.mdpi.com/2504-4990/6/4/131), [published repository](https://bitbucket.org/iAnalytica/ingredient-substitution), [observed commit](https://bitbucket.org/iAnalytica/ingredient-substitution/commits/f29517446dff50a5b1fbfba685c1e781d7f8c50b), [arXiv v1 PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2409.08792v1).
- **What this does and does not establish:** a paper-linked artifact exists now, and the currently visible commit predates the paper's stated access date by five days. That temporal proximity is consistent with the statement, but does not prove it was the exact accessed tree, that these files produced every reported table, or that the preprint's differently named repository was the same repository before a rename. A present-day 404 is not evidence of historical nonexistence. No repository was cloned, executed, or redistributed; no dataset, output, model weight, or code bytes were inspected or locally archived; no reuse or rights approval is inferred. The API result is a version locator, not an experimental reproduction.
- **L42 disposition:** use `f295174...` as the next version-pinned paper-to-artifact candidate, while tracking the arXiv/journal URL difference. Compare published split, filtering/judge flow, output files, and model/prediction correspondence with the paper; preserve uncertainty wherever the artifact cannot bind an output to a table. Exact novelty remains `UNKNOWN`; no author-reported score becomes local evidence.

## LEAD v202 — Add direct LLM/Recipe1MSubs prior art to L42 (2026-10-14)

- **Direct comparator found by citation chase:** Rita et al., “Optimizing Ingredient Substitution Using Large Language Models to Enhance Phytochemical Content in Recipes,” *Machine Learning and Knowledge Extraction* 6(4):2738–2752 (2024), DOI `10.3390/make6040131`, arXiv `2409.08792v1`. The arXiv v1 PDF was retrieved from the versioned arXiv endpoint (2,379,827 bytes; SHA-256 `9dbe664a6a9d9b131cd4587751397a92d1acc06dee2974e995d74e50a6c65601`; snapshot was not retained locally). Sources: [versioned arXiv full text](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2409.08792v1), [arXiv record](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.08792), [journal article](https://www.mdpi.com/2504-4990/6/4/131).
- **What the paper reports (author-reported, not reproduced):** Recipe1MSubs’ fixed 49,044/10,729/10,747 train/validation/test partition; recipe-context prompts; fine-tuned GPT-3.5-Turbo-1106, DaVinci-002, and TinyLlama-1.1B, compared with GISMo using Hit@1. On the original dataset the paper reports GPT-3.5 at `38.03 ± 0.28%` vs GISMo `34.53 ± 0.10%`; on its separately filtered dataset it reports `54.46 ± 0.29%` vs `40.24 ± 0.36%`. The filtering calls GPT-3.5-Turbo to classify substitutions across all partitions as Correct/Potential/Incorrect and retains Correct examples; the resulting test set has 6,721 examples rather than the original 10,747. The paper also reports phytochemical enrichment of 1,951 pairings / 1,639 recipes and explicitly cautions that disease relevance is preclinical, not clinical evidence.
- **Material scope effect:** This is direct prior art for LLM fine-tuning and recipe-context ingredient-substitute ranking on the same named benchmark, plus model-assisted substitution-validity filtering. Do not frame “LLM-based Recipe1MSubs substitution,” contextual LLM ranking, or model-assisted curation as an unoccupied space. The paper does **not**, on the inspected text, establish the frozen task’s repeated-vs-unseen-pair strata, selective risk/coverage, calibrated abstention, evidence-missingness strata, or provenance-bearing safety decision. The filtered-set score changes both the evaluation population and its judge-derived inclusion rule; it is not a same-test-set comparison with the original score, and neither result is an independently reproduced local metric. Exact novelty remains `UNKNOWN`; L42 stays open pending the full hashed funnel and independent adjudication.
- **Next / acceptance:** Pin and inspect the paper-linked Bitbucket artifact at the cited version (or record the access failure), then align its candidate identity, split overlap, judge/filter flow, prompt/task, and evaluation denominator against the frozen ChefKix protocol. Carry the paper as an adjacent/direct comparator in the claim matrix; do not claim it is a duplicate or dismiss it as irrelevant. No SSoT, protocol, scores, or claims-register status changed here.

- **Paper-side statement:** PubMed Central's article record says the complete CARE v2.0 codebase, prompt templates, and CAREBench-150 were publicly available at `SerinaRica/CARE`, accessed 2026-02-12. The paper reports 400k Recipe1M+ and CAREBench-150 evaluation. Keep both as the authors' stated provenance and reported results, not independently verified experiment evidence. Source: [article data-availability record](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC13206443/).
- **Repository-side dated evidence:** The visible `main` history has the core-code commit `186276c` dated 2026-02-02; the next visible commit `f217ab5` is dated 2026-04-04 and changes README branding only. Thus, **inference**: the linked `main` snapshot on the paper's stated 2026-02-12 access date appears to be `186276c`. That tree's experiment plan names a 17.5k Chinese recipe source and an intended 1,000+ enrichment; its tracked `test/test_queries.json` contains ten examples, not an identifiable 150-query CAREBench artifact. The checked-in README also says large recipe/nutrition files must be separately supplied. A 2026-10-14 read-only ref check found only `refs/heads/main` and no tags; targeted searches surfaced no separate release or CAREBench-150 mirror. This is an unsuccessful discovery, not proof that no external artifact exists. Sources: [2026-02-02 commit patch](https://github.com/SerinaRica/CARE/commit/186276c.patch), [2026-04-04 branding commit patch](https://github.com/SerinaRica/CARE/commit/f217ab50a53a6854861c77e7539f94a4146fb34a.patch), [experiment plan](https://github.com/SerinaRica/CARE/blob/186276c/docs/experiment_plan.md), [tracked query examples](https://github.com/SerinaRica/CARE/blob/186276c/test/test_queries.json), [README](https://github.com/SerinaRica/CARE/blob/f217ab50a53a6854861c77e7539f94a4146fb34a/README.md).
- **Meaning and limit:** This is a material, date-bounded code/data/version mapping gap between the article's availability statement and the identifiable artifacts in the linked main-branch tree. It does **not** prove the authors' dataset/results are false or unreproducible: a separately hosted release, unindexed artifact, omitted/private branch, later upload, or mistaken access date remains possible. Do not characterize CAREBench-150 as absent from every public channel without checking those alternatives.
- **L42 action/acceptance:** Seek the paper-linked release/tag and its commit/tree for the stated access date; map the CAREBench-150 150-item artifact, Recipe1M+ processing, prompt templates, and reported metric scripts to exact hashes. If unresolved, ask the authors through an approved channel and carry the unresolved correspondence into the claims register plus independent adjudication. Until then, preserve the article as adjacent prior art, do not equate the ChefMind harness with CARE v2.0, and keep exact novelty `UNKNOWN`.

Treat the contribution boundary as narrower, not as proof of novelty.

## The Five Contributions (C1–C5 from SSoT §3)

| ID | Contribution | Floor | Target | Stretch |
|----|--------------|-------|--------|---------|
| **C1** | **Instrument**: strata × allergen cases × valid@k audit | 208 allergen cases + Recipe1MSubs strata + 150-case 2-rater audit | + Vietnamese pilot (~100 cases, 2 native raters) | Public release with datasheet |
| **C2** | **Measurement campaign** across arms | GISMo + lookup + one API LLM | + Mistral Stage 3 + second API LLM; intervals everywhere | Contamination-aware 2×2 (public/private × Western/Vietnamese) |
| **C3** | **Guardrail**: hard allergen filter + per-cell conformal sets + abstention | Frozen protocol, honest run, cells reported | Informative sets in ≥1 cell; vacuous cells shown | Fewer allergen violations than raw arms with intervals; Ooi's abstract describes context-based allergy substitution, while prompt/RAG allergen substitution and restriction-aware recipe-context substitution are prior art, not novelty by themselves (LEAD v293/v297–v298). The 2019 co-occurrence-derived prohibited-ingredient list is not an authoritative allergen source or validated hard filter; its example analysis does not establish safety. The 2022 ontology models dietary conditions contextually, but schema-level capacity is not an operationalized or validated filter (v300). Exact fail-closed/provenance/conformal combination remains unadjudicated; candidate SafePlates code has a static no-marker parser concern, not validated safety evidence (v294); do not conflate the two source leads bundled as S23 (v295); generic reject-by-default/rationale alone is not a novelty claim (LEAD v292). Co-occurrence/category-based substitute recommendation also predates this work, but is not allergy validation (LEAD v299). |
| **C4** | **Human evidence** | 2-reviewer pass on audit pilot, κ (kappa) reported with cluster-level interval | LLM judge calibrated to humans (κ ≥ 0.6; kappa ≥ 0.6) | Usefulness study N ≥ 20 (N >= 20; ethics permitting); Vij et al. report an LLM allergen-safety score without human calibration in the screened text; SafePlates' interactive human correction is not independent judge calibration (v294); rationale-quality ratings are not substitute-label validity or judge-to-human calibration (LEAD v293/v292); keep the S23 source identities/results separate (v295) |
| **C5** | **Product proof** | Real-data Explorer + evidence endpoint + visible abstention | Live side-by-side demo vs raw LLM on same inputs | Reusable component |

**Bounded negatives (no further spend):** SAG, dual encoder, flavor pairing, CLIP projection, VLM artifact, behavioral overlay, voice copilot. One thesis chapter: *What did not work, and what each failure taught about the evaluation.*

**L13 re-scoped:** from "train new GNN" → "shift-aware selector over frozen scorers" (SSoT §3.5).

## Acceptance Gates per Contribution (SSoT §3.2)

| ID | Gate (all binary, checkable) |
|----|------------------------------|
| C1 | Instrument schema validated; strata files regenerate bit-for-bit from official splits; audit pilot labels for ≥150 cases exist with two independent raters and adjudication log; κ reported with case-level interval |
| C2 | Every arm evaluated on identical case list, identical normalization, identical scoring script; each number in a table has an interval; arms with unmatched-output rate >5% flagged |
| C3 | Protocol frozen and hashed before test access; calibration only on validation; per-cell status table (ok / vacuous / no_guarantee); cost-of-trust figure from frozen run |
| C4 | Rater agreement computed per §6.8 with cluster-level interval; judge-vs-human agreement per §7.5; if κ/kappa < 0.6 twice (kappa < 0.6 twice), fallback applied and stated |
| C5 | Explorer loads from hash-pinned graph artifact; demo runs from clean checkout without network except API arms; abstention state reachable and tested |

**Capacity arithmetic (SSoT §18; source estimates, not confirmed capacity):** 225 MUST + 32 SHOULD + 70 COULD = 327 lead hours; 91 member hours. Eight weeks at 30h/week = 240h (short 87h); at 40h/week = 320h (still 7h short before reviewer time and estimate overlap/tiering checks). Reconcile actual hours and seek resources/timing before any human-approved scope revision.

**Source compression scenarios and compression protocol (SSoT §18; human-decision alternatives, not active cuts):** Level 0 full plan; Level 1 postpones COULD except a qualitative native-language appendix; Level 2 limits calibration to rank arms/no judge, uses bounded n=208 allergen work and a minimal Explorer; Level 3 is the collapse/audit/allergen/negative/write-up floor. First seek additional people, compute, keys, time, and parallel work. If a fallback is explicitly approved, record removed proof, retained destination, and revisit trigger; never weaken scientific or ethics gates.

| ID | Risk | Likelihood | Impact | Early Warning | Mitigation |
|----|------|------------|--------|---------------|------------|
| K1 | Raters not found | M | H | Fewer than two named by proposed Oct 15 target; actual status unconfirmed (LEAD v217/v219) | Reconcile availability; seek resources/timing first (§18); any fallback requires explicit HUMAN decision and preserved gates |
| K2 | Rater agreement too low (κ/kappa < 0.5 twice; kappa < 0.6 twice review trigger) | M | H | T1 failure | Rewrite handbook once; any reduced audit requires explicit HUMAN decision and bounded claims |
| K3 | H1 inconclusive (CI straddles Δmin) | M | M | CI straddles 15pp | Expand only under predeclared analysis and explicit HUMAN approval before result review; otherwise report inconclusive |
| K4 | Judge fails gates (§7.5) | M | M | Gates fail | Use humans-only smaller calibration only if explicitly approved; state power loss |
| K5 | All cells vacuous | M | M | Early GISMo diagnostics | Report quantified limit; policy = abstain-by-default |
| K6 | Calibration/test not exchangeable | M | H | KS diagnostic on gold rank | Merge cells or weighted conformal; state limit |
| K7 | Validation scores missing | M | H | M-B check fails | Regenerate GISMo val (cheap); LLM arms on subsample |
| K8 | Stage 3 late/fails | M | M | No next authorized artifact boundary validated after Segment 9 step 561; Segment 10 last recorded `RUNNING` at 2026-10-14T05:50:49.607985Z (LEAD v175), historical and not a fresh poll | Require exact successor checkpoint/manifest validation; Stage-2 adapter hedge remains available |
| K9 | Inference too slow | M | M | 200-case timing | Stratified subsample (§13.3) |
| K10 | API keys/budget unavailable | M | H | No key by proposed Freeze A Oct 22 | Pursue authorized access/resources or adjust timing first; any alternate arm requires explicit HUMAN/protocol approval, pinned model identity, and revised claims boundary; no automatic spend or substitution |
| K11 | Ethics blocks usefulness study | M | L | No written disposition recorded in local notes by proposed Oct 12 target; actual status unconfirmed (LEAD v217/v219) | Escalate resource/schedule; do not automatically cancel H4 (§14) |
| K12 | Allergen n too small to certify | H | M | Arithmetic fixed; route choice and any added frame remain unapproved | HUMAN selects and freezes the prespecified ≥299 expansion or n=208 bounded-interval route before execution; no automatic fallback or safety conclusion |
| K13 | Claim breaks under review | M | H | Red-team finds forbidden | §24 forbidden-claims audit; claims register |
| K14 | Novelty duplicate found | L–M | H | L42 search | Present scoped alternatives to HUMAN; re-scope C1 only with explicit direction |
| K15 | Advisor says scope mismatch | L–M | H | U2 reply | Re-scope before more spend |
| K16 | No named, verified, willing qualified ARR contributor with capacity and complete profile by the internal readiness checkpoint | M | M | Candidate, qualification evidence, written consent/capacity, or profile checks are not present in the local record (LEAD v238); Dec 1 is an internal trigger, not a verified ARR deadline | Thesis only or later ARR cycle; do not assume a waiver or guaranteed review |
| K17 | Licenses block release | M | L–M | M-F, §10.2 | Release code/aggregates only |
| K18 | Scope creep | H | H | Work exceeds confirmed MUST scope | Review weekly against the ideal scope; pursue resources/timing first (§18); any cut requires explicit HUMAN decision and recorded consequence |
| K19 | Overnight job overspend | L | M | Receipt > cap | Per-job caps; refuse-to-start |
| K20 | Teammate unavailable | M | M | Tasks slip 2 weeks | Reassign independent work; pursue resources/timing; any scope change requires explicit HUMAN decision (§18) |
| K21 | R0 numbers leak into thesis | M | H | Figure without claim ID | Register rule (§15.2) |
| K22 | Non-thesis workload / unmeasured remaining capacity | H | H | No dated, package-level remaining-hours and confirmed-availability baseline is recorded locally (LEAD v240); the 327/91 source estimates are not remaining effort | Record actual availability and unfinished-package estimates; recompute pace/slack; pursue resources/timing first; any fallback requires explicit HUMAN decision and retained-proof statement |

**Pre-mortem triggers (SSoT §19.3 — reopen architecture only on these):**
- H1 lower Wilson bound ≥ Δmin → lead with benchmark validity
- H1 upper bound < Δmin → lead with collapse map + abstention
- H1 straddles Δmin → report inconclusive; extend only if time
- κ < 0.6 twice → rewrite handbook once; shrink to audited subset
- All cells vacuous → reframe C3 as quantified limit
- Novelty duplicate → re-scope C1
- Advisor mismatch → re-scope before spend
- Exchangeability diagnostic fails → weighted conformal or merge; state limit
- No ARR contributor by Dec 1 → thesis only

## Protocol Resolutions (from SSoT §16 — must resolve before affected work)

| Flag | Conflict | Resolution Required |
|------|----------|---------------------|
| PR01 | §6.1 conditional rescue vs §6.9 joint rescue; > vs ≥ Δmin | Choose estimand, denominator, operator prospectively |
| PR02 | Gold forced A yet both-rater N = label noise | Specify raw/adjudicated/gold policy; implications for V≥H |
| PR03 | Equal allocation vs population inference; top-4 vs top-5 | Resolve allocation, weighting, stratum minima, consistent k |
| PR04 | Cell uses gold substitute (unavailable at deployment) | Separate descriptive oracle strata from deployable partition |
| PR05 | Judge chosen on test audit; claim freeze before test | Establish dev/audit/cal/eval separation; disclose exposure |
| PR06 | Filter/rerank/truncation/empty-pool mismatch; infinity handling | Define returned-set success, filter ordering, guarantee population |
| PR07 | KS called exchangeability check; test-driven merging | Diagnostics cannot prove assumption; freeze variants or label exploratory |
| PR08 | Coverage grid .50–.95 vs α={.05,.1,.2,.3,.5} | Freeze single grid and status semantics |
| PR09 | Lossy normalization vs v23 collision findings | Validate identity/food-form behavior before freeze |
| PR10 | Different κ point/lower-bound gates; Wilson vs exact | State each gate's population/statistic/interval method |
| PR11 | Train-only graph vs "no val/test pair" despite seen overlap | Resolve row provenance leakage test without changing splits |
| PR12 | Endpoint example seen=true/count=0/unseen simultaneously | Replace only via validated contract design |
| PR13 | Annotation pending ethics vs clearance-before-study | Obtain institutional authorization; prep continues |
| PR14 | Seed placeholders marked VERIFIED; checker accepts dummy | Implement real artifact validation separately |
| PR15 | Synthetic tests omit bounded-M/infinite/missing-cell | Independent validation before reuse |

## Operating Rhythm (from SSoT §24)

- **Daily (10 min):** Morning review of receipts for overnight jobs (§24 rhythm; §13.6 manifest details)
- **Monday (30 min):** Claims register diff; overnight receipts; plan week against cut line
- **Wednesday (15 min):** Check gates due; chase people
- **Friday (30 min):** Update status block (generated); write one thesis paragraph from week's evidence; decide cuts
- **Biweekly advisor update (5 lines):** Evidence IDs, gates moved, plan changes/trigger, precise request, next action

**Freeze A (Oct 22):** Audit protocol, Δmin, strict/lenient, arms, blinding, normalization, primary arm/stratum, κ bar, timestamp/hash.

**Freeze B (Nov 6):** Deployable cells, n floor, M, α grid, labeling function/selection, filter/rank/set semantics, infinity/missing, informativeness, diagnostics/sensitivities, evaluation access. **Neither freeze completed here — resolve §16 first.**

**Forbidden claims (SSoT §24 — red-team every sentence):** No "zero/<1% violations" without n≥299; no "safe/safer/clinical"; no "human-valid coverage" from judge; no "first/novel" before 4.6+second reviewer; no Vietnamese guarantee; no "measured contamination"; no R0 figures without reconstruction; no cross-task rankings; no "four trained models/production-ready AI"; no "zero cost/local-only" without runtime proof; no user outcome claims; no untraceable numbers; no "calibrated" without cell/label/assumption (never claim "calibrated without" its cell, label, or assumption); no "hypothesis confirmed" when interval includes null.

---

## Evidence Integrity Rules

- Never mark COMPLETE because code was written — require executable evidence (checkpoint, hash-bound predictions, scored evaluation).
- Never compare cancelled run or paper number to locally executed result.
- Never report metric without dataset, split, protocol version, seed count.
- Negative results (SAG, dual encoder, CLIP, flavor, VLM, behavioral, voice) are thesis contributions — report honestly.
- Do NOT cite: 22.04% Senath (unverified journal abstract); 0.2222 HGAT (synthetic); 0.00% allergen (self-labeled, withdrawn).
- Every number in thesis carries a claim ID from register (§15). Unverified = say so.
